. Taking supremum over all
\phi\in H^{0.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
|\phi|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}\leq 1
, we have by the definition of
\dot{H}^{0.5}
-norm that

|\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}}\lesssim P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}).

(3.28)

Similarly as above, we have

\displaystyle|\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}),

(3.29)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|\partial_{t}^{3}\eta\|_{2},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{3}).

(3.30)

Combining (
3.24
)-(
3.30
) and the bound (
3.18
) for
\partial_{t}^{3}\eta
, we get

\|\partial_{t}^{3}\psi\|_{2}\lesssim P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}),

which is exactly (
3.15
). Hence, (
3.19
) directly follows from (
3.15
) and
\partial_{t}^{4}\eta=\partial_{t}^{3}(v+\psi)
.

∎

3.3
Estimates for the enthalpy
h

In this section we are going to control the Sobolev norm
\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}h\|_{k}
for
k=0,1,2,3
and
\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}
.

3.3.1
Estimates for the first order derivatives of
h

We need to control
\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|\partial_{t}h\|_{0}
. First, we have

\frac{\partial h}{\partial y^{\alpha}}=\delta^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h=\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h+(\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}h.

Invoking the a priori assumption (
3.8
) and the second equation in (
3.1
), we get

\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}+\epsilon\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq(g+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{\infty}})+\epsilon\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(3.31)

Therefore, for sufficiently small
\epsilon
, which can be achieved by choosing
T>0
smaller if needed, we have

\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim g+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(3.32)

Second, as for
\|\partial_{t}h\|_{0}
, we use the third equation of (
3.1
) and the physical assumption (
1.8
) to get

\|\partial_{t}h\|_{0}\lesssim\|\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{0}\lesssim\|\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial v\|_{0}\lesssim\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\|\partial v\|_{0}.

(3.33)

3.3.2
Estimates for the top order derivatives of
h

We take the Eulerian divergence (i.e.,
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}
) in the second equation of system (
3.1
) and use the third equation of (
3.1
) to get a wave equation of
h
:

\tilde{J}e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h-\partial_{\nu}(E^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\mu}h)=\underbrace{-\tilde{J}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}}_{:=F}-\tilde{J}e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2},

(3.34)

where
E^{\nu\mu}=\tilde{J}\tilde{a}^{\nu\alpha}\tilde{a}^{\mu}_{\alpha}
. Note that the matrix
E
is symmetric and positive-definite thanks to (
3.7
).

Let
\mathfrak{D}=\partial_{t}
or
\overline{\partial}{}
. Let
\mathfrak{D}^{3}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}
or
\partial_{t}^{3}
, i.e., all the 3rd-order tangential derivatives. Applying
\mathfrak{D}^{3}
to (
3.34
), we get

\tilde{J}e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h-\partial_{\nu}(E^{\nu\mu}\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{\mu}h)=\mathfrak{D}^{3}F\underbrace{-[\mathfrak{D}^{3},\tilde{J}e^{\prime}(h)]\partial_{t}^{2}h+\mathfrak{D}^{3}(\tilde{J}e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2})}_{F_{3}}+\partial_{\nu}([\mathfrak{D}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h).

(3.35)

Multiplying (
3.35
) by
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h
, then integrating
\partial_{\nu}
by parts, we have

\displaystyle~~~~~~~\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{J}e^{\prime}(h)|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{t}h|^{2}+E^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\partial_{\mu}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\,dy

(3.36)

\displaystyle=~~\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}E^{\nu\mu}\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{\nu}h\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{\mu}h\,dy

(3.37)

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{\Omega}F_{3}\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\,dy

(3.38)

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{3}F\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\,dy

(3.39)

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\nu}([\mathfrak{D}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\,dy.

(3.40)

(
3.37
) can be directly bounded by the energy:

\eqref{h4e1}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}E\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial h\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim P(\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{2},\|\partial\psi\|_{2},\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial h\|_{0})

(3.41)

To estimate (
3.38
), it suffices to bound
\|F_{3}\|_{0}
. The precise form of
F_{3}
is

F_{3}=\sum_{m=2}^{5}\sum e^{(m)}(h)(\partial_{t}^{i_{1}}\mathfrak{D}^{j_{1}}h)\cdots(\partial_{t}^{i_{m}}\mathfrak{D}^{j_{m}}h),

where the second sum is taken over the set
\{i_{1}+\cdots+i_{m}=2,~j_{1}+\cdots+j_{m}=3,~1\leq i_{m}+j_{m}\leq 4\}
. Invoking the condition imposed on
e(h)
(i.e., (
1.8
)), one has

\sum_{\mathfrak{D}^{3}}\|F_{3}\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0},\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3},\|\mathfrak{D}h\|_{L^{\infty}}).

(3.42)

As for (
3.39
), one has
\mathfrak{D}^{3}F=\mathfrak{D}^{3}(\tilde{J}\tilde{a}^{\nu\alpha}\tilde{a}^{\mu}_{\alpha})
.

•

When
\mathfrak{D}^{3}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}
, then
\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}(\tilde{J}\tilde{a}^{\nu\alpha}\tilde{a}^{\mu}_{\alpha})\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})
.

•

When
\mathfrak{D}^{3}
contains at least one time derivative, then

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}F\|_{0}

\displaystyle=\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}\partial_{t}(\tilde{J}\tilde{a}^{\nu\alpha}\tilde{a}^{\mu}_{\alpha})\|_{0}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}J\|_{2}\|a\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|J\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}a\|_{2}\|a\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim P(\|\partial v\|_{2},\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}}).

Therefore,

\eqref{h4e3}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3})\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\|_{0}

(3.43)

Finally, one has to be cautious when controlling (
3.40
). The leading order term in
\partial_{\nu}([\mathfrak{D}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)
is
\partial\mathfrak{D}^{3}E
. If
\mathfrak{D}^{3}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}
, then this term loses control in
L^{2}
. To avoid this problem, one can integrate
\partial_{\nu}
by parts, and then integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts in the time integral of (
3.40
) to replace
\partial_{\nu}
falling on
E
by
\partial_{t}
. This is because
J
and
\partial_{t}J
(also for
a
and
\partial_{t}a
) have the same spatial regularity. If
\mathfrak{D}^{3}
contains at least one time derivative, then the
L^{2}
-norm of
\partial\mathfrak{D}^{3}E
can be controlled directly thanks to the same reason above.

•

\mathfrak{D}^{3}
contains at least one time derivative, i.e.,
\mathfrak{D}^{3}=\mathfrak{D}^{2}\partial_{t}
. Then

\displaystyle~~~~\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\nu}([\mathfrak{D}^{2}\partial_{t},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\mathfrak{D}^{2}\partial_{t},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}\partial_{t}E\|_{1}\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}E\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2}+\|\mathfrak{D}\partial_{t}E\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathfrak{D}h\|_{2}+\|\mathfrak{D}E\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathfrak{D}\partial_{t}h\|_{1}+\|\partial_{t}E\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}h\|_{1})\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\|_{0}.

So

\sum_{\mathfrak{D}^{3}\backslash\{\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\}}\eqref{h4e4}\lesssim P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1})\|\partial_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h\|_{0}.

(3.44)

•

When
\mathfrak{D}^{3}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}
, we consider the time integral of (
3.40
). We first integrate
\partial_{\nu}
by parts, then integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts to get the following equality

\displaystyle~~~~\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\nu}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)N_{\nu}\underbrace{\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h}_{=0}~dS\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{\nu}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\,dt-\int_{\Omega}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{\nu}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\bigg|^{t=T}_{t=0}.

The leading order term in the first integral is
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}E
which has
L^{2}
- control, so one can bound this directly by using Hölder’s inequality

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{\nu}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial v\|_{3},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3})\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\|_{1}\,dt.

(3.45)

As for the second integral, we can use Hölder’s inequality first, then use
\epsilon
-Young’s inequality and Jensen’s inequality

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{\nu}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\bigg|_{t=T}

(3.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}E(T)\|_{0}\|\partial h(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}E(T)\|_{1}\|\partial\overline{\partial}{}h(T)\|_{1}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}+\|\overline{\partial}{}E(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\overline{\partial}{}^{2}h(T)\|_{0}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{8\epsilon}(\|\overline{\partial}{}E(T)\|_{2}^{4}+\|\overline{\partial}{}E(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}h(T)\|_{1}^{4})

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{8\epsilon}\left(\|\overline{\partial}{}E(0)\|_{2}^{4}+\|\overline{\partial}{}E(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4}+\|h(0)\|_{3}^{4}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}E(t)\|_{2}^{4}+\|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}h(t)\|_{1}^{4}\,dt\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3})\,dt.

The above estimates along with

\int_{\Omega}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{\nu}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\bigg|_{t=0}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}

give the bound for the time integral of (
3.40
):

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\nu}([\overline{\partial}{}^{3},E^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h)\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h\,dy\,dt\lesssim\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3})\,dt.

(3.47)

Now, summing up (
3.41
)-(
3.45
), (
3.47
) and then plugging it into (
3.36
), we get the tangential derivative estimates of
h
:

\sum_{\mathfrak{D}^{3}}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{J}e^{\prime}(h)|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{t}h|^{2}+|\partial\mathfrak{D}^{3}h|^{2}\,dy\bigg|^{t=T}_{t=0}\lesssim\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.48)

Note that we have used
E
is symmetric and positive-definite. Choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, the term
\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}
can absorbed by the LHS of (
3.48
).

3.3.3
Estimates for the full Sobolev norm

Up to now, we have controlled all the tangential space-time derivative of
\partial h
. Therefore it suffices to control
\geq 2
normal derivatives of
h
. Actually this follows directly from the wave equation (
3.34
)

e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h-\partial_{\nu}(E^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\mu}h)=\underbrace{-\tilde{J}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}}_{:=F_{0}}-e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}

that

\partial_{33}h=-\frac{1}{E_{33}}\left(F_{0}-e^{\prime\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}h)^{2}-\sum_{\nu+\mu\leq 5}\partial_{\nu}(E^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\mu}h)-e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\right),

because the above identity shows that the second order normal derivative
\partial_{33}h
can be bounded by the terms containing
h
with the same or lower order derivates and less normal derivatives. Hence, one can apply the same method to inductively control terms containing
h
with more normal derivatives. For example,
\partial_{3333}h
can be controlled in the same way by taking
\partial^{2}
in (
3.34
) and then express
\partial_{3333}h
in terms of the terms with same or lower order and
\leq 3
normal derivatives.

Therefore, combining with (
3.7
), (
3.32
), (
3.33
) and (
3.48
), one has the control for the Sobolev norm of enthalpy
h
and its time derivatives after taking
\epsilon>0
in (
3.48
) sufficiently small to be absorbed by
\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}
:

\left(\|h(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}h(t)\|_{4-k}^{2}+\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{4}h(t)\|_{0}^{2}\right)\bigg|_{t=0}^{t=T}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.49)

3.4
The div-curl estimates for
v

In this section we are going to do the div-curl estimates for
v
and its time derivatives in order to reduce the estimates of
\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}
to the tangential estimates. Recall the Hodge-type decomposition in (
2.7
):

\forall s\geq 1:~~\|X\|_{s}\lesssim\|X\|_{0}+\|\text{curl }X\|_{s-1}+\|\text{div }X\|_{s-1}+|X\cdot N|_{s-0.5}.

Let
X=v,\partial_{t}v,\partial_{t}^{2}v,\partial_{t}^{3}v
and
s=4,3,2,1
, respectively. We get

\displaystyle\|v\|_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }v\|_{3}+\|\text{curl }v\|_{3}+|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

(3.50)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}v\|_{2}+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}v\|_{2}+|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}(\partial_{t}v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1}+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1}+|\overline{\partial}{}(\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}+|\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot N|_{0.5}.

First, the
L^{2}
-norm of
v
is controlled by:

\|v(T)\|_{0}\leq\|v_{0}\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}v(t)\|_{0}\,dt,

(3.51)

while for
\|v_{t}\|_{0},\|v_{tt}\|_{0}
and
\|v_{ttt}\|_{0}
, we commute
\partial_{t}
through
\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h+ge_{3}
and obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\|_{0}\,dt\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{1})\,dt

(3.52)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1})\,dt

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{3}v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{3}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{0})\,dt.

Now we are going to control the curl term. Recall that
-\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h
is the Eulerian gradient of
h
whose Eulerian curl is 0. This motivates us to take Eulerian curl in the equation
\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h+ge_{3}
to get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v)_{\lambda}=\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}v^{\alpha},

(3.53)

where
(\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}X)_{\lambda}:=\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\tilde{a}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}X^{\alpha}
is the Eulerian curl of
X
and
\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}
is the sign of the 3-permutation
(\lambda\mu\alpha)\in S_{3}.

Taking
\partial^{3}
in the last equation and then taking inner product with
\partial^{3}\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v
, we get

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\partial^{3}\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v|^{2}=\int_{\Omega}(\partial^{3}\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v^{\lambda})\partial^{3}(\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}v^{\alpha})\,dy\lesssim P(\|v\|_{4},\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2}),

(3.54)

and thus

\|\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v(T)\|_{3}\lesssim P(\|v_{0}\|_{4})+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{4},\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2})\,dt.

(3.55)

The Lagrangian curl only differs from the Eulerian curl by a sufficiently small term which shall be absorbed in the LHS

\displaystyle\|\text{curl }v(T)\|_{3}

\displaystyle=\|\text{curl}_{I-\tilde{a}}v(T)\|_{3}+\|\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v\|_{3}

(3.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\text{Id}-\tilde{a}\|_{3}\|v\|_{4}+P(\|v_{0}\|_{4})+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{4},\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|v\|_{4}+P(\|v_{0}\|_{4})+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{4},\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\,dt.

Commuting
\partial_{t}^{k}
(
k=1,2,3
) though (
3.53
), we get the evolution equation for
\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v

\partial_{t}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v)_{\lambda}=\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}v^{\alpha})-\partial_{t}([\partial_{t}^{k},\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}]v)_{\lambda}.

Commuting
\partial^{3-k}
through the above equation, and then taking
L^{2}
inner product with
\partial^{3-k}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v)
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\partial^{3-k}\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\partial^{3-k}\left(\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}v^{\alpha})-\partial_{t}([\partial_{t}^{k},\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}]v)_{\lambda}\right)\partial^{3-k}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v)^{\lambda}\,dy

(3.57)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{0}\cdot\underbrace{\left\|\partial^{3-k}\left(\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{k}(\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}v^{\alpha})-\partial_{t}([\partial_{t}^{k},\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}]v)_{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{0}}_{=:D_{k}}.

One can use

\tilde{a}=[\partial\tilde{\eta}]^{-1}=\tilde{J}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{2}\tilde{\eta}\times\partial_{3}\tilde{\eta}\\
\partial_{3}\tilde{\eta}\times\partial_{1}\tilde{\eta}\\
\partial_{1}\tilde{\eta}\times\partial_{2}\tilde{\eta}\end{pmatrix},

\partial_{t}\eta=v+\psi
, and the estimates for
\psi
in Lemma
3.2
to control
D_{k}
directly. Note that the leading order terms in
D_{k}
are
\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k+1}\tilde{a}
and
\partial^{4-k}\partial_{t}^{k}v
. Therefore,

\sum_{k=1}^{3}D_{k}\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{3}P(\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k},\|\partial_{t}^{k}\psi\|_{4-k},\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}),

which implies

\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}\lesssim\epsilon\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.58)

For boundary terms in (
3.50
), we invoke the normal trace lemma (cf. Lemma
2.4
) to get

|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(v\cdot N)|_{0.5}\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\text{div }v\|_{0}.

(3.59)

Similarly we have

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}(\partial_{t}v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}v\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\text{div }\partial_{t}v\|_{0}

(3.60)

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}(\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}\text{div }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}

(3.61)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot N|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}.

(3.62)

Therefore the boundary estimates are all reduced to divergence and tangential estimates.

Now we come to estimate the divergence. Recall that the Eulerian divergence
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}X=\text{div }X+(\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}-\delta^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}X_{\alpha}
, which together with (
3.7
) implies

\displaystyle\forall s>2.5

\displaystyle:~\|\text{div }X\|_{s-1}\lesssim\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}X\|_{s-1}+\|I-\tilde{a}\|_{s-1}\|X\|_{s}\lesssim\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}X\|_{s-1}+\epsilon\|X\|_{s}

(3.63)

\displaystyle\forall 1\leq s\leq 2.5

\displaystyle:~\|\text{div }X\|_{s-1}\lesssim\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}X\|_{s-1}+\|I-\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|X\|_{s}\lesssim\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}X\|_{s-1}+\epsilon\|X\|_{s}.

The
\epsilon
-terms can be absorbed by
\|X\|_{s}
on LHS by choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. So it suffices to estimate the Eulerian divergence which satisfies
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v=-\partial_{t}e(h)
. Taking time derivatives in this equation, we get

\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v=-\partial_{t}^{k+1}e(h)-[\partial_{t}^{k},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}]\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha},~~k=0,1,2,3.

The leading order terms in
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}v
are
e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{k}h\partial_{t}h
,
\partial_{t}^{k}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}
and
\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{k-1}v_{\alpha}
when
k\geq 1
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v\|_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h\|_{3}

(3.64)

\displaystyle\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}v\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2}+\|\partial v\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim P(\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2},\|v\|_{3})

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2})+P(\|v_{0}\|_{3})+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial_{t}v(t)\|_{3})\,dt

and similarly,

\displaystyle\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1},\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{2})

(3.65)

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1},\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2})\,dt

\displaystyle\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0},\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1},\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0},\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1},\|e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1})\,dt.

Combining (
3.63
) and (
3.64
)-(
3.65
), we know the divergence estimates are all be reduced to the estimates of
h
which has been done in Section
3.3
. By choosing
\epsilon>0
in (
3.63
) to be sufficiently small, and using the estimates of
h
in (
3.49
), we finally finish the divergence estimates

\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.66)

3.5
Estimates for time derivatives of
v

As a result of div-curl estimates, it suffices to estimate the
L^{2}
-norms of
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}v,\cdots,\partial_{t}^{4}v
. In this part we are going to do the tangential estimates for the time derivatives of
v
, in order to finish the control
\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}
with
k\geq 1
. The fact that
\partial^{2}\eta
and
\partial_{t}\partial^{2}\eta
are of the same spatial regularity in Sobolev norms is essential for us to close the estimates.

Let
\mathfrak{D}^{4}=\partial_{t}^{4},\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{},\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}
. First we compute

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{D}^{4}v|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha}\mathfrak{D}^{4}\partial_{t}v^{\alpha}\,dy=-\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha}\mathfrak{D}^{4}(\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h)\,dy

(3.67)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}(\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha})\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}(\partial_{\mu}\mathfrak{D}^{4}h)\,dy-\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha}([\mathfrak{D}^{4},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}]\partial_{\mu}h)\,dy}_{L_{1}}.

In the first integral above, we integrate
\partial_{\mu}
by parts and invoking the equation
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v=-e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h
to obtain:

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}(\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha})\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}(\partial_{\mu}\mathfrak{D}^{4}h)\,dy

(3.68)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}N_{\mu}\underbrace{\mathfrak{D}^{4}h}_{=0}dS\underbrace{-\int_{\Omega}([\mathfrak{D}^{4},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}]\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha})\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\,dy}_{L_{2}}+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\,dy}_{L_{3}}+\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{4}\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\,dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}\mathfrak{D}^{4}(e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h)\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\,dy+L_{2}+L_{3}

\displaystyle=-\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)|\mathfrak{D}^{4}h|^{2}\,dy+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h|\mathfrak{D}^{4}h|^{2}-[\mathfrak{D}^{4},e^{\prime}(h)]\partial_{t}h\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\,dy}_{L_{4}}+L_{2}+L_{3}.

It is not difficult to see
L_{3}
and
L_{4}
can be controlled directly:

\displaystyle L_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}v\|_{0}\|\partial a\|_{2}\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t)),

(3.69)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathfrak{D}^{4}}L_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\|_{0}P\left(\sum_{k\geq 1}\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{4-k}h\|_{0}\right)\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t)).

(3.70)

To estimate
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
, it suffices to control the commutator
[\mathfrak{D}^{4},\tilde{a}]f
in
L^{2}
-norm.

\displaystyle\|[\mathfrak{D}^{4},\tilde{a}]f\|_{0}

\displaystyle=\|(\mathfrak{D}^{4}a)f+4(\mathfrak{D}^{3}a)(\mathfrak{D}f)+6(\mathfrak{D}^{2}a)(\mathfrak{D}^{2}f)+4(\mathfrak{D}a)(\mathfrak{D}^{3}f)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}a\|_{0}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}a\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{D}f\|_{1}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}a\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}f\|_{1}+\|\mathfrak{D}a\|_{2}\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}f\|_{0}.

Let
f=\partial v
and
\partial h
respectively (corresponding to
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
), and recall
a=[\partial\eta]^{-1}
. By Lemma
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}v\|_{0}(\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}a\|_{0}\|\partial v\|_{2}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}a\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{D}\partial v\|_{1}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}a\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{D}\partial v\|_{1}+\|\mathfrak{D}a\|_{2}\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial v\|_{0})

(3.71)

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\sum_{k=1}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k},\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|v\|_{4})\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t));

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}h\|_{0}(\|\mathfrak{D}^{4}a\|_{0}\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}a\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{D}\partial h\|_{1}+\|\mathfrak{D}^{2}a\|_{1}\|\mathfrak{D}\partial h\|_{1}+\|\mathfrak{D}a\|_{2}\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial h\|_{0})

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{0},\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2})\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t)).

Summing up (
3.67
)-(
3.71
), we are able to get the energy bound

\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h)}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}h\|_{0}^{2}\right)\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t)).

(3.72)

3.6
Estimates for spatial derivatives of
v
: Alinhac’s good unknown method

Now it remains to control
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2}
to close the a priori estimates of the approximation system (
3.1
). It should be emphasized here that our method in Section
3.5
cannot be used in the full spatial derivatives, because the
L^{2}
-norm of the commutator
[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\tilde{a}](\partial v)
and
[\overline{\partial}{}^{4},\tilde{a}](\partial h)
cannot be controlled due to the lack of time derivatives. To overcome such difficulty, we introduce Alinhac’s good unknowns for both
v
and
h
, which actually uncover that the essential leading order terms in
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v
and
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h
is exactly the covariant derivative
\nabla_{\tilde{a}}
of their Alinhac’s good unknowns. As a result, one can commute
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
and
\nabla_{\tilde{a}}
in the energy estimate without producing any higher order commutator apart from
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h
. However, the third equation of (
3.1
) yields
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h=-\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h
, which gives the energy term
-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h\|_{0}^{2}
and thus no extra higher order term appears. This being said, the use of Alinhac’s good unknowns avoids the control of the 5-th order wave equation of
h
together with delicate elliptic estimates, e.g., Lindblad-Luo
[
39
]
, Luo
[
42
]
, Ginsberg-Lindblad-Luo
[
20
]
.

The Alinhac’s good unknown was first introduced by Alinhac
[
3
]
, and has been frequently used in the study of free-boundary problems of incompressible fluids because the incompressibility condition (Eulerian divergence-free) eliminates the only extra term
\overline{\partial}{}^{r}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v)=0
, e.g., Masmoudi-Rousset
[
44
]
, Gu-Wang
[
22
]
, Wang-Xin
[
61
]
, etc.
On the other hand, in free-boundary problems of compressible fluids, the Alinhac’s good unknowns were crucial in
[
56
]
together with the Nash-Moser iteration. Moreover, there are several studies for free-boundary problems in ideal compressible MHD equations in which the passage to the Alinhac’s good unknowns is used to study the linearized problem in the framework of the Eulerian approach. For example, in this connection, we refer to
[
6
,
54
,
55
]
for compressible current-vortex sheets, and
[
47
,
57
,
58
]
for the plasma-vacuum interface problem in compressible MHD.

3.6.1
Introducing Alinhac’s good unknowns

For simplicity we replace
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}
by
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}
which is more convenient for us to deal with the correction term
\psi
on the boundary. For a smooth function
g=g(t,x)
, we define its “Alinhac’s good unknown” (for the 4-th order derivative) to be

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}g=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g,

(3.73)

which enjoys the following important properties.

Lemma 3.4
.

We have

\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}g)=\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}\mathbf{G}+C^{\alpha}(g)

(3.74)

with

\|C^{\alpha}(g)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(3.75)

Proof.

Invoking the identity

\displaystyle\partial\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}=-\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\partial\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha},

(3.76)

which is obtained from differentiating
\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\eta_{\beta}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}
, then

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}g)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g)+(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}g+[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}g]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g)-\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{}(\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha})\partial_{\mu}g+[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}g]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g)-\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\partial_{\mu}g-([\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{},\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\partial_{\mu}g+[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}g]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\partial_{\mu}g)}_{=\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}\mathbf{G}}+\underbrace{\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta_{\gamma}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\gamma}g)-([\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{},\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\partial_{\mu}g+[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}g]}_{=:C^{\alpha}(g)},

where
[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},f,g]:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(fg)-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(f)g-f\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(g)
.
A direct computation yields that

\displaystyle\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta_{\gamma}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\alpha}(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}^{\gamma}g)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2})(\|\partial g\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial^{2}g\|_{2});

\displaystyle\|([\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{},\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\partial_{\mu}g\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{},\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\|_{0}\|g\|_{W^{1,\infty}}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})(\|\partial g\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial^{2}g\|_{1})

\displaystyle\|[\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}g]\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})(\|\partial g\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial^{2}g\|_{2}).

∎

Moreover,

\|\mathbf{G}\|_{0}^{2}
controls
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}g\|_{0}^{2}
modulo a controllable error term. Specifically,

Lemma 3.5
.

We have

\displaystyle\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}g(T)\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\|\mathbf{G}(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial g\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}g\|_{L^{\infty}}).

(3.77)

Proof.

The definition of
\mathbf{G}
implies

\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}g(T)\|_{0}\approx\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}g(T)\|_{0}\lesssim\|\mathbf{G}(T)\|_{0}+\left\|\Big(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g\Big)\big|_{t=T}\right\|_{0},

where

\left\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g\big|_{t=T}\right\|_{0}\leq\left\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g\big|_{t=0}\right\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g)\|_{0}.

Here,
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g|_{t=0}=0
because
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta_{0}=0
. For the integrand of the second term, invoking (
3.76
) with
\partial=\partial_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g)=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{v}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g+\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}g-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\partial_{\tau}\tilde{v}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\tau\beta}\partial_{\mu}g

whose
L^{2}
-norm can be controlled by
P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial g\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}g\|_{L^{\infty}}).

∎

Remark
.

For general initial data
\eta_{0}
, the term
P(\|\eta_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|\partial g(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}
should also appear on the RHS of (
3.77
). Specifically,

\left\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}g|_{t=0}\right\|_{0}\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta_{0}\|_{0}\|\partial\eta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial g(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq P(\|\eta_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|\partial g(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}.

3.6.2
Tangential estimates of
v
: Interior part

Now we introduce the Alinhac’s good unknowns for
v
and
h

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}v-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v

(3.78)

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h.

(3.79)

Applying
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}
to the second equation in system (
3.1
) and then using (
3.78
), (
3.79
) to get

\partial_{t}\mathbf{V}=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}}\mathbf{H}+\underbrace{\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v)-C(h)}_{=:\mathbf{F}},

(3.80)

subject to the boundary condition

\mathbf{H}=-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\partial_{3}h~~~\text{on }\Gamma,

(3.81)

with the continuity equation

\nabla_{\tilde{a}}\cdot\mathbf{V}=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v)-C^{\alpha}(v_{\alpha})~~~\text{in }\Omega.

(3.82)

Thanks to Lemma
3.5
, it suffices to bound
\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\mathbf{H}\|_{0}^{2}
to close the estimates for
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h\|_{0}^{2}
. Taking
L^{2}
inner product between (
3.80
) and
\mathbf{V}
, one gets

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\mathbf{V}|^{2}\,dy=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}\mathbf{H}\cdot\mathbf{V}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{F}\cdot\mathbf{V}\,dy.

(3.83)

The second term on the RHS of (
3.83
) can be directly controlled

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{F}\cdot\mathbf{V}\,dy

\displaystyle\leq(\|\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v)\|_{0}+\|C(h)\|_{0})\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}

(3.84)

\displaystyle\lesssim(P(\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta\|_{0},\|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}v\|_{0},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3})+P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}).

For the first term in RHS of (
3.83
), we integrate by part and use (
3.81
), (
3.82
) to get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}\mathbf{H}\cdot\mathbf{V}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{H}\cdot\mathbf{V}_{\alpha}\,dy

(3.85)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}(\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}N_{\mu}\mathbf{V}_{\alpha})dS+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{H}(\nabla_{\tilde{a}}\cdot\mathbf{V})\,dy+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{\mu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\mathbf{H}\mathbf{V}_{\alpha}\,dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\mathbf{V}_{\alpha}dS+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{H}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v)\,dy\underbrace{-\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{H}C^{\alpha}(v_{\alpha})+\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{\mu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\mathbf{H}\mathbf{V}_{\alpha}\,dy}_{L_{0}}

\displaystyle=:I+K+L_{0}.

First,
L_{0}
can be directly controlled by
P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa})
by using (
3.75
)

L_{0}\lesssim\|\mathbf{H}\|_{0}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|v\|_{4}+\|\partial\tilde{a}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{H}\|_{0}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}).

(3.86)

Then we use
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v=-e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h
to bound
K

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{H}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v)\,dy=-\int_{\Omega}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(e^{\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h)\,dy

(3.87)

\displaystyle=-\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h|^{2}\,dy+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime\prime}(h)\partial_{t}h|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h|^{2}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\mathbf{H}([\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta},e^{\prime}(h)]\partial_{t}h)\,dy

\displaystyle\underbrace{+\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h\,dy}_{:=K^{*}}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h|^{2}\,dy+K^{*}+P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|v\|_{4},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}).

The term
K^{*}
cannot be bounded directly because it contains a higher order term
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}h
, but we can consider its time integral and integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts, then using
\epsilon
-Young inequality to absorb the
\epsilon
-term.

\int_{0}^{T}K^{*}(t)\,dt=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\,dy\,dt=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\,dy\,dt

(3.88)

Integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts, we get

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\,dy\,dt=-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\,dy\bigg|^{t=T}_{t=0}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h)\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\,dy.

The second term on the RHS is controlled directly by
\int_{0}^{T}P(\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|v\|_{4})\,dt
. For the first term on the RHS, we have

\displaystyle\left|-\int_{\Omega}e^{\prime}(h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h)(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h)\,dy\bigg|^{t=T}_{t=0}\right|\lesssim P(\|h_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v_{0}\|_{4})+\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}\|\partial^{2}\eta(T)\|_{2}\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Using
\epsilon
-Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle~~~~\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}\|\partial^{2}\eta(T)\|_{2}\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{8\epsilon}(\|\partial^{2}\eta(T)\|_{2}^{4}+\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{4})

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{4},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3})\,dt\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}K^{*}(t)\,dt\lesssim\epsilon\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{4},\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3})\,dt.

(3.89)

Here,
\epsilon\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}^{2}
can be moved to the LHS when
\epsilon
is sufficiently small. This concludes the control of
K
.

3.6.3
Tangential estimates of
v
: Boundary part

Now it remains to control the boundary term
I
, where the Taylor sign boundary term in
\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}
is produced and the correction term
\psi
exactly eliminates the extra out-of-control terms produced by the tangential smoothing (these terms are 0 if
\kappa=0
).

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\mathbf{V}_{\alpha}~dS

(3.90)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}v_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v_{\alpha})~dS

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}\eta_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\psi-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v_{\alpha})~dS.

We construct the Taylor-sign term in the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}
from the first term.

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}\eta_{\alpha}~dS

(3.91)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS

\displaystyle+\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta})([\Lambda_{\kappa},\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}]\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}\eta_{\alpha})\,dS

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h|\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}|^{2}\,dS-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\partial_{3}h|\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}|^{2}\,dS

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS}_{B_{1}}+\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta})([\Lambda_{\kappa},\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}]\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\partial_{t}\eta_{\alpha})\,dS}_{LB_{1}}.

In
LB_{1}
, we integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}
by parts (by interpreting it in the Fourier sense) and then use Sobolev trace lemma, (
2.32
) and Lemma
3.3
to get

\displaystyle LB_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{1.5}\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta})\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}([\Lambda_{\kappa},\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}]\overline{\partial}{}(\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{t}\eta_{\alpha}))\,dS

(3.92)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2}|\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{t}\eta_{\alpha}|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2}\|\partial^{2}h\|_{2}\|\partial\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v+\psi\|_{4}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial^{2}h\|_{2}).

Next, we plug
\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}=-\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}
into
B_{1}
and then separate the normal derivative of
\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}
from tangential derivatives.

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS}_{LB_{2}}+\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS

(3.93)

\displaystyle=LB_{2}+\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\psi_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS}_{LB_{3}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS}_{B_{1}^{*}}.

LB_{2}
can be directly bounded

\displaystyle LB_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}^{2}|\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}|_{L^{\infty}}

(3.94)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}^{2}P(\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}})\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}).

In
LB_{3}
, the term
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}
cannot be directly bounded, but we can use (
2.28
) in Lemma
2.6
to control this term by
(1/\sqrt{\kappa})|\eta|_{3.5}
.

\displaystyle LB_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\alpha}|_{L^{\infty}}|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}|\overline{\partial}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}|\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{}\eta|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}P(\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|v\|_{3},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}})|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}.

(3.95)

The factor
1/\sqrt{\kappa}
can be eliminated by plugging the expression of
\psi
in (
3.2
). We apply Sobolev embedding
W^{1,4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
first, and note that
\overline{\partial}{}\psi=P_{\geq 1}(\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{}^{-1}(\cdots))
does not contain the low-frequency part, which (actually follows from the Littlewood-Paley characterization of
W^{1,4}
and
\dot{W}^{1,4}
) implies
|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{W^{1,4}}\approx|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{\dot{W}^{1,4}}\approx|\overline{\Delta}{}\psi|_{L^{4}}
. Hence, we have

|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim|\overline{\Delta}{}\psi|_{L^{4}}=\left|\mathbb{P}\underbrace{\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v\right)}_{f}\right|_{L^{4}}.

According to the Littlewood-Paley characterization of
L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and the almost orthogonality property, we know

\displaystyle|\mathbb{P}f|_{L^{4}}\approx\left|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}|\tilde{P}_{N}P_{\geq 1}f|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|_{L^{4}}\approx\left|\left(\sum_{N\geq 0}|\tilde{P}_{N}f|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|_{L^{4}}\lesssim\left|\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}|\tilde{P}_{N}f|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right|_{L^{4}}\approx|f|_{L^{4}},

where
\tilde{P}
is the Littlewood-Paley projection with respect to
\tilde{\chi}(\cdot):=\chi(2\cdot)
.

Remark
.

For more details of Littlewood-Paley characterization of Sobolev spaces, we refer readers to Chapter 1.3 in Grafakos
[
21
]
or Appendix A in Tao
[
52
]
.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v\right|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\overline{\Delta}{}(\eta_{\beta}-\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta})\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}(v-\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v)\right|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\overline{\Delta}{}(\eta_{\beta}-\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta})|_{L^{\infty}}|\tilde{a}^{i\beta}|_{L^{\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v|_{0.5}+|\overline{\Delta}{}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}|_{0.5}|\tilde{a}^{i\beta}|_{L^{\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}(v-\Lambda_{\kappa}v)|_{L^{\infty}},

where in the last step we use
H^{0.5}\hookrightarrow L^{4}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}.
Now, recall (
2.29
) in Lemma
2.6
that we are able to control
|\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}-\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\overline{\Delta}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}}
by
\sqrt{\kappa}|\overline{\Delta}{}\eta|_{1.5}\leq\sqrt{\kappa}\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2}.
Similarly,
|\overline{\partial}{}(v-\Lambda_{\kappa}v)|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}|\overline{\partial}{}v|_{1.5}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|v\|_{3}
. Therefore, one has

|\overline{\partial}{}\psi|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3}),

and

LB_{3}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}})|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}).

(3.96)

As for
B_{1}^{*}
, it cannot be directly bounded, but together with another term they will be exactly eliminated by the correction term in (
3.90
).

Now we start to control the third term in (
3.90
). Again we separate the normal derivative of
v
from tangential derivatives

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}}v_{\alpha}~dS

(3.97)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\partial_{3}v_{\alpha}~dS\underbrace{-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}~dS}_{B_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}(-\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\partial_{3}v_{\alpha})(\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta})(\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\,dS+B_{2}^{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}|_{0}^{2}P(\|v\|_{3},\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}})+B_{2}^{*}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa})+B_{2}^{*},

where in the last step we control
|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}|_{0}
as follows

\displaystyle|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq|\Lambda_{\kappa}(\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta})|_{0}+|[\Lambda_{\kappa},\tilde{a}^{3\beta}]\overline{\partial}{}(\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta})|_{0}

(3.98)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda_{\kappa}(\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta})|_{0}+|\tilde{a}|_{W^{1,\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\eta|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}})\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}).

So far, what remains to be bounded is the second term in RHS of (
3.90
)

I_{2}:=-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\psi\,dS,

(3.99)

and

B_{1}^{*}=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{3\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}\,dS

(3.100)

and

B_{2}^{*}=-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}~dS

(3.101)

Plugging the expression of
\psi
in (
3.2
) into (
3.99
), one has

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}(\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{ir}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v_{\alpha})\,dS

(3.102)

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS

(3.103)

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}([\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}]\overline{\Delta}{}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\,dS

(3.104)

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}P_{<1}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v\right)\,dS.

(3.105)

It is clear that (
3.103
) exactly cancels with
B_{2}^{*}
in (
3.101
), and (
3.104
) can be directly bounded

\displaystyle\eqref{I230}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}|_{L^{\infty}}|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}|[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}]\overline{\Delta}{}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}|_{0}

(3.106)

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4},|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0})\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}).

For (
3.105
), one can apply Bernstein’s inequality (
2.23
) in Lemma
2.5
and (
3.98
) to get

\displaystyle\eqref{I240}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}|_{L^{\infty}}|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}\left|P_{<1}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v\right)\right|_{\dot{H}^{2}}

(3.107)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}|_{L^{\infty}}|\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}|_{0}\cdot\left|\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v\right|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}).

For (
3.102
), we try to move one
\Lambda_{\kappa}
on
\eta_{\beta}
to
\eta_{\alpha}
to produce the cancellation with
B_{1}^{*}
in (
3.100
):

(
3.102
)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}(\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v_{\alpha})(\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\gamma})\,dS

(3.108)

\displaystyle~~~~-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\beta}([\Lambda_{\kappa},\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\tilde{a}^{ir}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v_{\alpha}]\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\eta_{\gamma})\,dS

(3.109)

\displaystyle~~~~-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\tilde{a}^{3\beta}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}([\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{i\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v_{\alpha}]\overline{\Delta}{}\eta_{\gamma})\,dS

(3.110)

Now we see that (
3.108
) exactly cancels with
B_{1}^{*}
in (
3.100
). The terms in (
3.109
) can be controlled by using the mollifier property (
2.32
) after integrating
\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}
by part (similar to the estimates of
LB_{1}
), and (
3.110
) can be directly controlled by using Sobolev trace lemma. We omit the detailed computation here.

\eqref{I212}+\eqref{I213}\lesssim P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}).

(3.111)

Finally, summing up (
3.91
)-(
3.101
), (
3.111
) and plugging it into (
3.90
), we get the estimate for the boundary term
I
after using the Taylor sign condition
\partial_{3}h\leq-c_{0}/2<0
:

\int_{0}^{T}I(t)\,dt\lesssim\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h|\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}|^{2}\,dS+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt\lesssim-\frac{c_{0}}{4}|\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt

(3.112)

Now, summing up (
3.83
), (
3.84
), (
3.85
), (
3.86
), (
3.89
) and (
3.112
), we get the estimates for the Alinhac’s good unknowns

\|\mathbf{V}(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h(T)\|_{0}^{2}+|\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.113)

Finally, from the property of Alinhac’s good unknowns (
3.77
), we can get the estimates of
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v
that

\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v(T)\|_{0}^{2}+\|e^{\prime}(h)\overline{\partial}{}^{4}h(T)\|_{0}^{2}+|\tilde{a}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\Lambda_{\kappa}\eta_{\alpha}|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.114)

3.7
Closing the
\kappa
-independent a priori estimates

We conclude this section by deriving the uniform-in-
\kappa
a priori bound for the energy functional
\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}
of approximation system (
3.1
). Let
\mathcal{T}(t):=-\frac{1}{\partial_{3}h(t)}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathcal{T}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}=\|\mathcal{T}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{3}\partial_{t}h(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\mathcal{T}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}.

(3.115)

This implies that the physical sign condition can be propagated if
\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}
remains finite. Next, by plugging (
3.50
), (
3.51
), (
3.52
), (
3.56
), (
3.58
), (
3.59
)-(
3.62
), (
3.66
), (
3.72
) and (
3.114
) into (
3.3
), with
\epsilon>0
chosen sufficiently small, together with the estimates for
\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial\eta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v(t)+\psi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,dt\lesssim\|\partial\eta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v(t)\|_{2}+\|\psi(t)\|_{2}\,dt,

(3.116)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial^{2}\eta_{0}\|_{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v(t)\|_{2}+\|\psi(t)\|_{2}\,dt,

(3.117)

we get

\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(T)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}_{\kappa}(t))\,dt.

(3.118)

Now, (
3.4
) follows from (
3.118
) and the Gronwall-type inequality in Tao
[
52
]
, which finishes the proof of Proposition
3.1
.

4
Construction of the solution to the approximation system

The goal of this section is to construct the solution to the
\kappa
-approximation (nonlinear) system (
3.1
) by an iteration of the approximate solutions
\{(v^{(n)},h^{(n)},\eta^{(n)})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
. We start with
(v^{(0)},h^{(0)},\eta^{(0)})=(v^{(1)},h^{(1)},\eta^{(1)})=(0,0,\text{Id})
. Inductively, given
(v^{(n)},h^{(n)},\eta^{(n)})
for some
n\geq 1
, we construct the
(n+1)
-th approximate solutions
(v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)},\eta^{(n+1)})
from the linearization of (
3.1
) near
a^{(n)}:=[\partial\eta^{(n)}]^{-1}
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta^{(n+1)}=v^{(n+1)}+\psi^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}v^{(n+1)}=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}-ge_{3}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}v^{(n+1)}=-e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}h^{(n+1)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
h^{(n+1)}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
(\eta^{(n+1)},v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)})|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},v_{0},h_{0}).\end{cases}

(4.1)

Here
\tilde{a}^{(n)}:=[\partial\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}]^{-1}
and the correction term
\psi^{(n)}
is determined by (
3.2
) with
\eta=\eta^{(n)},v=v^{(n)},\tilde{a}=\tilde{a}^{(n)}
in that equation. Specifically, we need following facts for the linearized approximation system (
4.1
) to construct a solution to the
\kappa
-approximation (nonlinear) system (
3.1
):

•

System (
4.1
) has a (unique) solution (in a suitable function space).

•

The solution of (
4.1
) constructed in the last step has an energy estimate uniformly in
n
.

•

The approximate solutions
\{(v^{(n)},h^{(n)},\eta^{(n)})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
converge strongly (in some Sobolev spaces).

4.1
A priori estimates for the linearized approximation system

Before we construct the solution of (
4.1
), we would like to derive the uniform-in-
n
a priori estimates for this system. Define the energy functional for (
4.1
) to be

\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t):=\|\eta^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}v^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\left(\|h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{k}^{2}\right)+W^{(n+1)}(t),

(4.2)

where
W^{(n+1)}
is the energy functional for the 5-th order wave equation of
h^{(n+1)}

W^{(n+1)}(t):=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{5-k}h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{3}^{2}.

(4.3)

Remark
.

The last two terms in (
4.3
) can be simplified to
\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}(t)\|_{4}^{2}
if
\Omega
is bounded. In this case, the wave energy becomes
W^{(n+1)}=\sum_{k=0}^{4}(\|\partial_{t}^{5-k}h^{(n+1)}\|_{k}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}\|_{k}^{2})
.

Our conclusion is

Proposition 4.1
.

For the solution
(v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)},\eta^{(n+1)})
of (
4.1
), there exists
T_{\kappa}>0
sufficiently small, depending only on
\kappa>0
such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}.

(4.4)

Remark
.

As we will see in the following computation, the control of 4-th order derivatives of
v
and
h
does not need the energy of 5-th order wave equation of
h
; the only important difference from the a priori estimates for (
3.1
) is the boundary term (
4.42
) for which we apply the property of tangential smoothing to give a direct control with an extra factor
1/\kappa
, instead of producing subtle cancellation as in Section
3.6
.
However, we included
W^{(n+1)}
in
\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}
since we need this constraint when constructing the function space when proving the existence of the solution to the linearized system.

We prove Proposition
4.1
by induction on
n
. First, when
n=-1,0
, then the conclusion automatically holds because of
(v^{(0)},h^{(0)},\eta^{(0)})=(v^{(1)},h^{(1)},\eta^{(1)})=(0,0,\text{Id})
. Suppose uniform bound holds for all positive integers
\leq n-1
. Then from the induction hypothesis, one has

\forall k\leq n,~~\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}^{(k)}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}.

(4.5)

We would like to first simplify our notation before we derive the energy estimate for
(v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)},\eta^{(n+1)})
. We denote
(v^{(n)},h^{(n)},\eta^{(n)})
by
(\mathring{v},\mathring{h},\mathring{\eta})
and
\mathring{a}:=[\partial\mathring{\eta}]^{-1}
,
\mathring{J}:=\det[\partial\mathring{\eta}]
; and
(v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)},\eta^{(n+1)})
by
(v,h,\eta)
. The smoothed version of
\mathring{a},\mathring{\eta},\mathring{J}
are denoted by
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}},\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}},\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}
respectively. Besides, we define
\sigma:=e^{\prime}(\mathring{h})
. Now, the linearized system (
4.1
) becomes

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta=v+\mathring{\psi}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h-ge_{3}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v=-\sigma\partial_{t}h~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
h=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
(\eta,v,h)|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},v_{0},h_{0}).\end{cases}

(4.6)

We note that the initial data of (
4.6
) is the same as the original system (
1.13
) because
\mathring{a}|_{t=0}=a|_{t=0}=I
.

4.1.1
Uniform-in-
n
bounds for the coefficients

The energy functional for
\mathring{v},\mathring{h},\mathring{\eta}
reads

\mathring{\mathcal{E}}:=\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\mathring{v}\|_{k}^{2}+\left(\|\mathring{h}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\mathring{h}\|_{k}^{2}\right)+\mathring{W},

(4.7)

where
\mathring{W}
is the energy functional for the 5-th order wave equation of
\mathring{h}
, i.e.,

\mathring{W}:=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{5-k}\mathring{h}\|_{k}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{(n-1)}}\mathring{h}\|_{k}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{(n-1)}}\mathring{h}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{(n-1)}}\mathring{h}\|_{3}^{2}.

(4.8)

We have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathring{\mathcal{E}}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}

(4.9)

in light of the induction hypothesis (
4.5
).

In addition, we have the following bounds for
\mathring{a},\mathring{\eta},\mathring{J}
provided they hold for
(v^{(k)},h^{(k)},\eta^{(k)})
for
k\leq n-1
. The control of these quantities are important when we do the uniform-in-
n
a priori estimates and construct the solution for system (
4.6
).

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
T\in(0,T_{\kappa})
. There exists some
0<\epsilon<<1
and
N>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathring{\psi}

\displaystyle\in L_{t}^{\infty}([0,T];H^{4}(\Omega)),~~\partial_{t}^{l}\mathring{\psi}\in L_{t}^{\infty}([0,T];H^{5-l}(\Omega)),~~\forall 1\leq l\leq 4;

(4.10)

\displaystyle\|\mathring{J}-1\|_{3}

\displaystyle+\|\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}-1\|_{3}+\|\text{Id}-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\|_{3}+\|\text{Id}-\mathring{a}\|_{3}\leq\epsilon;

(4.11)

\displaystyle\partial\mathring{\eta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\infty}(\Omega)),\quad\partial^{2}\mathring{\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\Omega));

(4.12)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathring{\eta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{4}(\Omega));

(4.13)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{l+1}\mathring{\eta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{5-l}(\Omega)),~~\forall 1\leq l\leq 4;

(4.14)

\displaystyle\mathring{J}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{\infty}(\Omega)),~~\partial\mathring{J}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\Omega);

(4.15)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathring{J}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{3}(\Omega)),~~\partial_{t}^{l+1}\mathring{J}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{4-l}(\Omega)),~~\forall 1\leq l\leq 4;

(4.16)

\displaystyle 1/N\leq\sigma\leq N,

\displaystyle~\partial_{t}^{l}\sigma\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{5-l}(\Omega)),~~\forall 1\leq l\leq 5.

(4.17)

Particularly, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Bigg(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\mathring{\eta}(t)\|_{4}+\sum_{1\leq l\leq 4}\|\partial_{t}^{l+1}\mathring{\eta}(t)\|_{5-l}+\|\mathring{J}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial\mathring{J}(t)\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}\mathring{J}(t)\|_{3}+\sum_{1\leq l\leq 4}\|\partial_{t}^{l+1}\mathring{J}\|_{4-l}+\sum_{1\leq l\leq 5}\|\partial_{t}^{l}\sigma(t)\|_{5-l}\Bigg)\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}.

(4.18)

Proof.

First, the bound for
\mathring{\psi}
and
\partial_{t}^{l}\mathring{\psi}
for
1\leq l\leq 3
directly follows from (
3.12
)-(
3.15
) in Lemma
3.3
. Then the identity

\text{Id}-\mathring{a}=-\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\mathring{a}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{a}:(\partial\partial_{t}\mathring{\eta}):\mathring{a}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathring{a}:(\partial(\mathring{v}+\psi^{(n-1)})):\mathring{a}

yields (
4.11
) by choosing
\epsilon
suitably small (depending on
T_{\kappa}
). Similar results hold for
\mathring{J}
.

As for
\mathring{\eta}
,
\partial_{t}\mathring{\eta}=\mathring{v}+\psi^{(n-1)}
gives (
4.12
) and (
4.13
). Taking
\partial_{t}^{l}
in this equation and combining the induction hypothesis on
\mathring{E}
and
\psi^{(n-1)}
we can get the bound for
\partial_{t}^{l+1}\mathring{\eta}
in (
4.14
). For
\mathring{J}
, recall
\mathring{J}:=\det[\partial\mathring{\eta}]
which equals a multi-linear funtion of its elements
\partial\mathring{\eta}
. So the bound for
\mathring{\eta}
and
\partial_{t}^{l}\mathring{\eta}
yields the bounds for
\partial_{t}^{l}\mathring{J}
.

To conclude the proof, it suffices to control
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}\|_{1}
. From (
3.20
), we know

\begin{cases}\Delta\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}=0&~~~\text{in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}=\overline{\Delta}{}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{v}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{v}\right)&~~~\text{on }\Gamma.\end{cases}

(4.19)

Using Lemma
2.3
for harmonic functions, we know

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}|_{0.5}=\left|\overline{\Delta}{}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{v}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{v}\right)\right|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathbb{P}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{v}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{v}\right)|_{\dot{H}^{-1.5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathbb{P}\partial_{t}^{4}\left(\overline{\Delta}{}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{v}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\mathring{v}\right)|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}},

where we used the Bernstein inequality (
2.24
) and the definition of
\mathbb{P}
(restrict
|\xi|\gtrsim 1
to get the last inequality).

The most difficult terms appear when
\partial_{t}^{4}
falls on
\overline{\Delta}{}\mathring{\eta}
or
\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{v}
. Here we only show how to control
\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v}
and the rest highest order terms can be controlled in the same way. For any test function
\phi\in\dot{H}^{0.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
|\phi|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}\leq 1
, we consider

\displaystyle|\langle\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v},\phi\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v},\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\phi\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v},\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}(\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\phi)\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim|\partial_{t}^{4}v|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}|\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\phi|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1}(|\phi|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}|\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}|_{L^{\infty}}+|\phi|_{L^{4}}|\overline{\Delta}{}\mathring{\eta}|_{\dot{W}^{0.5,4}}|\mathring{a}|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1}\|\partial^{2}\mathring{\eta}\|_{2}\|\partial\mathring{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}})|\phi|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}},

where we used
\dot{H}^{-0.5}
-
\dot{H}^{0.5}
duality and Kato-Ponce inequality (
2.20
). Taking supremum over all
\phi\in\dot{H}^{0.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with
|\phi|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}\leq 1
, we obtain

\|\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v}\|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v}\|_{1}\|\partial^{2}\mathring{\eta}\|_{2}\|\partial\mathring{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}},

and thus gives the bound for
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}\|_{1}
.
From the second equation of (
4.1
) we know that
\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v}=-\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n-1)}}\mathring{h}
, of which the
H^{1}
-norm of the RHS is exactly in the energy functional
\mathcal{E}^{(n-1)}
as in (
4.2
)-(
4.3
). So
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\psi}\|_{1}
is bounded by the induction hypothesis.

It remains to control
\|\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\eta}\|_{1}
which also gives the bounds for
\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{J}
. Taking
\partial_{t}^{4}
in the first equation of (
4.6
) we have
\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\eta}=\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{v}+\partial_{t}^{4}\psi^{(n-1)}
and
\partial_{t}^{4}\psi^{(n-1)}
can be bounded in
H^{1}
in the same way as above. Lastly, (
4.2
) follows from the estimates above and (
4.9
).
∎

4.1.2
Uniform-in-
n
a priori estimates for the linearized system

With the inductive hypothesis (
4.5
) and Lemma
4.2
, we are now able to control the energy functional for
(v,h,\eta)
which solves the system (
4.6
). Let

\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}:=\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}v\|_{k}^{2}+\left(\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}h\|_{k}^{2}\right)+W^{(n+1)},

(4.20)

where
W^{(n+1)}
