is the energy functional for the 5-th order wave equation of
h

W^{(n+1)}:=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{5-k}h\|_{k}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{k}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{3}^{2}.

(4.21)

The estimate for
\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}-W^{(n+1)}
is quite similar (actually a bit easier) to what we have done in Sect.
3
, so we will not go over all the details, but still point out the different steps, especially the boundary term control, because we no longer need
\kappa
-independent estimates.

Step 1: Estimates for
h

The lower order term
\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}
can be treated identically as what appears in Section
3.3.1
. The arguments in Sections
3.3.2
-
3.3.3
suggest that the key to control the top order Sobolev norm of
h
is to study the wave equation

\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}h-\partial_{\mu}(\mathring{E}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\mu}h)=-\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}-\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h,\quad\text{where}\,\,\mathring{E}^{\nu\mu}:=\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha},

(4.22)

which is obtained by taking
\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}
in the second equation of (
4.6
). Let
\mathfrak{D}=\overline{\partial}{}
or
\partial_{t}
. We take
\mathfrak{D}^{3}
in (
4.22
) to get

\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{D}^{3}h-\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{E}^{\nu\mu}\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{\mu}h)=-\mathfrak{D}^{3}(\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha})-[\mathfrak{D}^{3},\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h+\mathfrak{D}^{3}(\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)+\partial_{\nu}([\mathfrak{D}^{3},\mathring{E}^{\nu\mu}]\partial_{\mu}h).

(4.23)

Compared with (
3.35
), we only replace
e^{\prime}(h)
,
\tilde{a}
and
\tilde{J}
by
\sigma
,
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
and
\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}
, respectively. Using the same method and the a priori bound in Lemma
4.2
, one can get

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathfrak{D}^{3}}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{J}e^{\prime}(h)|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial_{t}h|^{2}+|\partial\mathfrak{D}^{3}h|^{2}\,dy\bigg|_{t=T}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}+\sum_{\mathfrak{D}^{3}}\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial^{2}\mathring{\eta}\|_{2},\|\partial\mathring{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{3},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1})\|\mathfrak{D}^{3}\partial h\|_{0}\,dt,

where
\epsilon>0
can be chosen sufficiently small such that
\epsilon\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h(T)\|_{1}^{2}
can be absorbed by LHS.
Finally, the full Sobolev norm
\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}h\|_{k},~k=0,1,2,3
and
\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}
can be bounded by adapting the arguments in Section
3.3.3
.

Step 2: The div-curl estimates for
v

From (
4.11
), (
4.13
) and (
4.14
) in Lemma
4.2
, we know all the steps can be copied as in Section
3.4
after replace
\tilde{a}
by
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
,
\eta
by
\mathring{\eta}
and
e^{\prime}(h)
by
\sigma
. We omit the detailed computations and only list the results here.

•

L^{2}
-estimates:

\displaystyle\|v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{0}\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}v(t)\|_{0}\,dt,

(4.25)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(t)\|_{0}\,dt\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{1})\,dt,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1},\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1})\,dt,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{3}v(T)\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{3}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}v\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{0})\,dt.

•

Boundary estimates:

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}(\partial_{t}v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}v\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\text{div }\partial_{t}v\|_{0}

(4.26)

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}(\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot N)|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}\text{div }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}

(4.27)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot N|_{0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}.

(4.28)

•

The div-curl estimates:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t))\,dt.

(4.29)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3-k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4-k}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)-W^{(n+1)}(t))\,dt.

(4.30)

Combining with Hodge’s decomposition inequality in Lemma
2.7
, to estimate the full Sobolev norm of
\partial_{t}^{k}v
, it suffices to control
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{k}\partial_{t}^{4-k}\|_{0}
.

Step 3: Tangential estimates for time derivatives of
v

This part also follows in the same way as Section
3.5
. Let
\mathfrak{D}^{4}=\partial_{t}^{4},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2},\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
. One can directly compute
\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\mathfrak{D}^{4}h|\,dy
and follow the same method in (
3.67
)-(
3.71
) to get the analogous conclusion as (
3.72
):

\sum_{k=1}^{4}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}^{4-k}h\|_{0}^{2}\right)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\cdot P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)-W^{(n+1)}(t)).

(4.31)

Step 4: Tangential estimates for
v

In this step we still mimic the proof as in Section
3.6
.
For a given function
g
, we use
\mathring{\mathbf{G}}
to denote its Alinhac’s good unknown. Then there holds

\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}g)=\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{G}}+\mathring{C}(g),

(4.32)

where the error term
\mathring{C}(g)
is defined in the same way as in (
3.74
) but replacing
\tilde{a}
by
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
. In view of (
3.75
), we have

\|\mathring{C}(g)\|_{0}\lesssim P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}})\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}}.

(4.33)

Similar to (
3.77
), one also has

\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}g(T)\|_{0}\lesssim\|\mathring{\mathbf{G}}(T)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|\mathring{v}\|_{4},\|\partial g\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}g\|_{L^{\infty}}).

(4.34)

We introduce the Alinhac’s good unknown
\mathring{\mathbf{V}}
and
\mathring{\mathbf{H}}
for
v
and
h
:

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathbf{V}}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}v-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v,

(4.35)

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle:=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}h-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h.

(4.36)

Applying
\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}
to the second equation in the linearization system (
4.6
), one gets

\partial_{t}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}=-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}+\underbrace{\partial_{t}(\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\cdot\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v)-\mathring{C}(h)}_{=:\mathring{\mathbf{F}}},

(4.37)

subject to the boundary condition

\mathring{\mathbf{H}}=-\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}_{\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\beta}\partial_{3}h~~~\text{ on }\Gamma,

(4.38)

and the corresponding compressibility condition

\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}=\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v)-\mathring{C}^{\alpha}(v_{\alpha}),~~~\text{in }\Omega.

(4.39)

Now we take
L^{2}
inner product between (
4.37
) and
\mathring{\mathbf{V}}
to get analogous result to (
3.83
).

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}|^{2}\,dy=-\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathbf{F}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy,

(4.40)

where
\|\mathring{\mathbf{F}}\|_{0}
can be directly controlled as in (
3.84
). As for the first term, we integrate by parts to get

-\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}}\,dy=-\int_{\Gamma}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\alpha}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{\alpha}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\,dS+\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{V}})\,dy+\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\mu}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{\alpha}\,dy,

(4.41)

where the second and the third term can be controlled in the same way as in (
3.85
)-(
3.89
).

For the boundary term in (
4.41
), we no longer need to plug the precise form of
\mathring{\psi}
into it and find the subtle cancellation as in Section
3.6
because the energy estimate is not required to be
\kappa
-independent. Instead, we integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}
by parts, apply Kato-Ponce inequality (
2.20
) and Sobolev embedding
{H}^{0.5}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
to get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\alpha}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{\alpha}\mathring{\mathbf{H}}\,dS

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta})\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\alpha}\mathring{\mathbf{V}}_{\alpha}\,dS

(4.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(|\partial_{3}h\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\alpha}|_{L^{\infty}}|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta})|_{0.5}|+|\partial_{3}h\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\beta}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{3\alpha}|_{W^{0.5,4}}|\overline{\partial}^{2}\overline{\Delta}(\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}_{\beta})|_{L^{4}}\big)|\mathring{\mathbf{V}}|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}(|\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}|_{L^{\infty}}+|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}|_{0.5}^{2})\frac{1}{\kappa}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\eta\|_{1}(|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}+|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}|\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\partial v|_{L^{\infty}}+|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{4}}|\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\partial v|_{\dot{W}^{0.5,4}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\kappa}P(\|\mathring{\eta}\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}},\|v\|_{4}).

Combining (
4.34
) with the estimates above, we have

\|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}v(T)\|_{0}\lesssim_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)-W^{(n+1)}(t))\,dt.

(4.43)

Summing up the estimates for
h
, div-curl estimates and tangential estimates, we get

\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}v\|_{k}^{2}+\Big(\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}h\|_{0}^{2}\Big)\lesssim_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)-W^{(n+1)}(t))\,dt.

(4.44)

4.1.3
Estimates for
W^{(n+1)}
: 5-th order wave equation of
h

We would like to control

W^{(n+1)}=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}^{5-k}h\|_{k}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{k}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{3}^{2}.

It suffices to control only the top order terms. We take
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}
in the second equation of (
4.6
) to get the wave equation for
h
:

\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}h-\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h=-\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}-\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h.

(4.45)

Before we derive the higher order wave equation, we would like to reduce the estimates of
W^{(n+1)}
to that of
\|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}^{2}
via (
4.45
) and the elliptic estimate Lemma
2.8
.

We start with
\|\partial^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}
and
\|\partial^{4}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}
. By the elliptic estimate Lemma
2.8
, we have

\|\partial^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}\lesssim C(\|\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{\mathcal{H}})(\sum_{r\leq 3}\|\partial^{r}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{3}\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}),

(4.46)

in which the term
\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{3}\lesssim\kappa^{-1}\|\partial^{2}\mathring{\eta}\|_{2}
by the property of tangential smoothing. The term
\partial^{3}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h
can be expressed as follows by using (
4.45
)

\partial^{3}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h=\partial^{3}(\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}h)+\partial^{3}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h),

(4.47)

which produces one more time derivative and thus reduce the control of
\partial^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h
to
\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h
:

\|\partial^{3}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h\|_{0}\leq\|\sigma\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|[\partial^{3},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}\|_{3}+\|\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h\|_{3}.

(4.48)

As for
\partial^{4}\partial_{t}h
, we note that for any
1\leq r\leq 4
,

(\partial^{r}f)_{\alpha}=\partial^{r-1}\partial_{\alpha}f=\partial^{r-1}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}f)+\partial^{r-1}((\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}f)

together with
\|\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}-1\|\leq\epsilon
gives

\|\partial^{r}f\|_{0}\lesssim\|\partial^{r-1}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}f\|_{0}+\epsilon\|\partial^{r}f\|_{0},

(4.49)

where the last term can be absorbed by LHS after choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficently small.

Therefore we have

\partial^{3}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{t}h=\partial^{3}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}h)+\partial^{3}(\underbrace{(\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha})}_{\|\cdot\|_{3}\leq\epsilon}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}h),

(4.50)

which gives

\|\partial^{4}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}\lesssim\|\partial^{3}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}+\epsilon\|\partial^{4}\partial_{t}h\|_{0},

(4.51)

where the last term can be absorbed by LHS after choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. So we are able to reduce the estimates for
\|\partial^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}
and
\|\partial^{4}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}
to
\|\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}
and
\|\partial^{3}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}
, respectively, plus lower order terms. In other words, we replace one spatial derivative by one time derivative via the elliptic estimate and wave equation (
4.45
).

Next, since
\partial_{t}h|_{\Gamma}=0
, we apply the elliptic estimate in Lemma (
2.8
) to
\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}h
to get

\|\partial^{3}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}\lesssim C(\|\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{\mathcal{H}})(\sum_{r\leq 2}\|\partial^{r}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}+\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}\|_{3}\|\partial_{t}h\|_{3}).

(4.52)

The term
\partial^{r}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}\partial_{t}h
can be re-expressed as follows by commuting
\partial_{t}
through (
4.45
):

\partial^{2}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}\partial_{t}h=\sigma\partial^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h+\partial^{2}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)+[\partial^{2}\partial_{t},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h-\partial^{2}([\partial_{t},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h),

(4.53)

and thus the control of
\partial^{2}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}\partial_{t}
is reduced to
\sigma\partial^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h
plus the other terms on the RHS of the last inequality

\|\partial^{2}\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}\partial_{t}h\|_{0}\lesssim\|\sigma\partial^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)\|_{2}+\|[\partial^{2}\partial_{t},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|[\partial_{t},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h\|_{2}.

(4.54)

As for
\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h
, we again rewrite one Lagrangian spatial derivative in terms of one Eulerian spatial derivative plus an error term:

\partial^{2}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}h=\partial^{2}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{2}h)+\partial^{2}((\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{2}h),

(4.55)

which gives

\|\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}\lesssim\|\partial^{2}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\epsilon\|\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0},

(4.56)

where the last term can be again absorbed by LHS after choosing
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small.

The reduction mechanism above can be summarized as the following diagram

\displaystyle\partial^{4}\partial_{t}h

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\eqref{trick}}\partial^{3}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\xrightarrow[\eqref{hwave0r}]{\text{Lem }\ref{GLL}}\partial^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}h\xrightarrow{\eqref{trick}}\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{3}h\xrightarrow[\eqref{hwave0r}]{\text{Lem }\ref{GLL}}\partial_{t}^{5}h;

(4.57)

\displaystyle\partial^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\eqref{hwave0r}]{\text{Lem }\ref{GLL}}\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}h\xrightarrow{\eqref{trick}}\partial^{2}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{2}h\xrightarrow[\eqref{hwave0r}]{\text{Lem }\ref{GLL}}\partial\partial_{t}^{4}h\xrightarrow{\eqref{trick}}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h.

As is shown above, we are able to replace one spatial derivative by one time derivative after using the elliptic estimate and wave equation (
4.45
). Repeat the steps above, we can reduce the estimates of
W^{(n+1)}
to
\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}
and
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}
which can be controlled via the 5-th order wave equation of
h
(i.e., taking
\partial_{t}^{4}
in (
4.45
)) plus commutator terms. Specifically,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{5-k}\partial^{k}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\partial^{k}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(\|\partial\mathring{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathring{\eta}\|_{2})(\sigma+\sigma^{2})(\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}+\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0})

(4.58)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\kappa}C(\|\partial\mathring{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial^{2}\mathring{\eta}\|_{2})(\sigma+\sigma^{2})(\|h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}h\|_{0}+\cdots+\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{0})

(4.59)

\displaystyle+\|[\partial^{3},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}\|_{3}+\|\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h\|_{3}

(4.60)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)\|_{2}+\|[\partial^{2}\partial_{t},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|[\partial_{t},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h\|_{2}

(4.61)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{2}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)\|_{1}+\|[\partial\partial_{t}^{2},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h\|_{1}

(4.62)

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{3}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)\|_{0}+\|[\partial_{t}^{3},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{0}+\|[\partial_{t}^{3},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h\|_{0}

(4.63)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{4}\|\partial^{k}([\partial_{t}^{4-k},\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha}]\partial_{\mu}h)\|_{0}^{2}

(4.64)

Here, all the commutator and error terms (
4.59
)-(
4.64
) consists of
\leq 4
derivatives of
v,\eta,h
, and
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
which have no problem to bound. Thus,

\eqref{comm1}+\cdots+\eqref{comm6}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\left(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)-W^{(n+1)}(t)\right),

(4.65)

where the RHS is controlled in (
4.44
).

It remains to control
\|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{0}
. We apply
\partial_{t}^{4}
to (
4.45
) to get:

\sigma\partial_{t}^{6}h-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)=\underbrace{-\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha})-\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)-[\partial_{t}^{4},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h+[\partial_{t}^{4},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h}_{=:F_{5}}.

(4.66)

Multiplying (
4.66
) by
\partial_{t}^{5}h
and integrate over
\Omega
, we get

\int_{\Omega}\sigma\partial_{t}^{5}h\partial_{t}^{6}h\,dy-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{5}h\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)\,dy=\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}F_{5}\partial_{t}^{5}h\,dy}_{LW_{1}}.

(4.67)

The first term in (
4.67
) is

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\sigma|\partial_{t}^{5}h|^{2}\,dy-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\sigma|\partial_{t}^{5}h|^{2}\,dy.

(4.68)

For the second term in (
4.67
), we integrate
\partial_{\nu}
by parts and note that
\partial_{t}^{5}h|_{\Gamma}=0
makes the boundary integral vanish.

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{5}h\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)\,dy

(4.69)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{5}h)(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)\,dy+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{5}h\partial_{\nu}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)\,dy}_{LW_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h|^{2}\,dy+\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}([\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha},\partial_{t}]\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)\,dy}_{LW3}+LW_{2}.

Plugging (
4.68
) and (
4.69
) into (
4.67
), we have

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\Omega}\sigma|\partial_{t}^{5}h|^{2}+|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h|^{2}\,dy\right)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\sigma|\partial_{t}^{5}h|^{2}\,dy+LW_{1}+LW_{2}+LW_{3}.

(4.70)

Now we come to estimate the RHS of (
4.70
): First, invoking (
4.17
), we have

\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\sigma|\partial_{t}^{5}h|^{2}\,dy\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\|\sqrt{\sigma}\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}^{2}.

(4.71)

Second, invoking (
4.2
), we have

LW_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0},

(4.72)

LW_{3}=-\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha})(\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)\,dy\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}.

(4.73)

We can write
\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}h=\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h+(\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h
and invoke
|\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}-\text{Id}|\leq\epsilon
to get
\|\partial\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}\lesssim\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}
. In consequence,

LW_{3}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}^{2}.

(4.74)

It remains to estimate
LW_{1}
, i.e.,
\|F_{5}\|_{0}
.

•

\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha})\|_{0}
: There are two terms containing 5 derivatives:
\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}
and
\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}
. The rest terms are of
\leq 4
derivatives and hence controlled. By (
4.2
) we know that
\|\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}\|_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}
, which gives
\|\partial_{t}^{5}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}\|_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{2}.
As for the second term, we invoke the second equation of (
4.6
) to get
\partial_{t}^{4}\partial v=-\partial_{t}^{3}\partial(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h+ge_{3})=-\partial_{t}^{3}\partial(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h)
, so we have

\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha})\|_{0}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\left(\|\partial v\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial(\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h)\|_{0}\right).

(4.75)

•

\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)\|_{0}
: Expanding all the terms, and then use the previous estimates for
\leq 4
derivative and invoking (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h)\|_{0}

(4.76)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{5}\sigma\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\sigma\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\sigma\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}(\|\partial_{t}h\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{1}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}h\|_{0}+\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}).

Also, one can control
[\partial_{t}^{4},\sigma]\partial_{t}^{2}h
in exactly the same way, so we omit the details.

•

[\partial_{t}^{4},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h
: A direct computation gives

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}^{4},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h))-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h))-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h)+\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h)-\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}h)

\displaystyle=[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}]\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h)+\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}([\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}]\partial_{\mu}h)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{4}(\partial_{t}^{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha})(\partial_{t}^{4-l}\partial_{\nu}(\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h))+\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu}_{\alpha}\partial_{\nu}\left((\partial_{t}^{l}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\mu}_{\alpha})(\partial_{t}^{4-l}\partial_{\mu}h)\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}^{4},\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}]h\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{a}\|_{0}\|\eta\|_{\mathcal{H}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{a}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{a}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{1}+\|\partial_{t}\mathring{a}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{1}

(4.77)

\displaystyle+\|\mathring{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}(\|\partial\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{a}\|_{0}\|\partial h\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{a}\|_{0}\|\partial\partial_{t}h\|_{2}+\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}\mathring{a}\|_{1}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}h\|_{1}+\|\partial\partial_{t}\mathring{a}\|_{2}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{3}h\|_{0})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\cdot(P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}-W^{(n+1)})+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{1}).

Combining (
4.75
)-(
4.77
), one has

LW_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{\sigma}}\mathcal{P}_{0}\cdot P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)})\|\partial_{t}^{5}h\|_{0}.

(4.78)

Summing up (
4.70
), (
4.71
), (
4.72
), and (
4.74
), we get the estimates for the wave equation (
4.66
)

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\Omega}\sigma|\partial_{t}^{5}h|^{2}+|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\partial_{t}^{4}h|^{2}\,dy\right)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\cdot P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)).

(4.79)

Therefore we finish the control of
W^{(n+1)}
by (
4.58
),(
4.65
) and (
4.79
)

\frac{d}{dt}W^{(n+1)}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\cdot P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)).

(4.80)

4.1.4
Uniform-in-
n
a priori estimates for the linearized approximation system

From (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.44
) and (
4.80
), we get

\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(T)\lesssim\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(0)+\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}P(\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t))\,dt,

(4.81)

which gives the uniform-in-
n
a priori estimates

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}^{(n+1)}(t)\lesssim_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}_{0}

for the linearized approximation system (
4.6
) (also for (
4.1
)) with the help of Gronwall-type inequality in Tao
[
52
]
.

\square

4.2
Construction of the solutions to the linearized approximation system

In this subsection we are going to construct the solutions to the linearized approximation system (
4.6
):

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta=v+\mathring{\psi}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega;\\
\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h-ge_{3}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega;\\
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v=-\sigma\partial_{t}h~~~&\text{ in }\Omega;\\
h=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma;\\
(\eta,v,h)|_{t=0}=(\text{Id},v_{0}.h_{0}),\end{cases}

given that
\mathring{\eta},\mathring{a},\mathring{\psi},\sigma
satisfying Lemma
4.2
.

4.2.1
Function space and Solution map

Definition

(Norm, Function space and Contraction)

We define the norm

\|\cdot\|_{Z^{r}}:=\sum_{s=0}^{r}\sum_{k+l=s}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial^{l}\cdot\|_{0}

and define the function space

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}(M,T):=

\displaystyle\bigg\{(\xi,w,\pi):(w,\xi)|_{t=0}=(v_{0},\text{Id}),

(4.82)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle\left(\|w(t),\partial_{t}\pi(t)\|_{Z^{4}}+\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t),\partial_{t}\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t)\|_{Z^{3}}+\|\partial_{t}\xi(t)\|_{Z^{3}}+\|\partial^{2}\xi(t)\|_{Z^{2}}+\|\partial\xi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\leq M\bigg\}.

We notice here that for given
M>0,T>0
,
\mathbb{X}(M,T)
is a Banach space.

Remark
.

As mentioned in the remark after (
2.18
), the quantity
\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t),\partial_{t}\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t)\|_{Z^{3}}
can be replaced by
\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\pi(t)\|_{Z^{4}}
if
\Omega
is bounded.

We then define the solution map
\Xi:\mathbb{X}(M,T)\to\mathbb{X}(M,T)
by

\displaystyle\Xi:\mathbb{X}(M,T)

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{X}(M,T)

(4.83)

\displaystyle(w,\pi,\xi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(v,h,\eta).

The image
(v,h,\eta)
is defined as follows:

1.

Define
\eta
by

\partial_{t}\eta=w+\mathring{\psi},~~~\eta(0)=\text{Id}.

(4.84)

2.

Define
v
by

\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi-ge_{3},~~~v(0)=v_{0}.

(4.85)

3.

Define
h
by the solution of the following wave equation

\begin{cases}\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}h-\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h=-\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}-\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}h~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
h=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
(h,\partial_{t}h)|_{t=0}=(h_{0},h_{1}).\end{cases}

(4.86)

The existence of this linear wave equation can be shown by adapting the method provided in Lax-Phillips
[
34
]
after turning it into a system of hyperbolic equations. Also, this solution lies in the space
\mathbb{X}(M,T)
owing to (
4.98
) in the upcoming subsection.

Here, we have to show that a solution for (
4.84
)-(
4.86
) with
(w,\pi,\xi)=(v,h,\eta)
implies a solution for (
4.6
). It suffices to show that we can recover the third equation of (
4.6
). First, since
\pi=h
, (
4.85
) reads

\partial_{t}v=-\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h-ge_{3}.

We take
\text{div}\,_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}
on both sides and get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{div}\,_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}v)+\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h=\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}.

(4.87)

Moreover, (
4.86
) implies
\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}h=\partial_{t}(\sigma\partial_{t}h)+\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}
, and by plugging this to (
4.87
) we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v+\sigma\partial_{t}h)=0

(4.88)

and hence
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v+\sigma\partial_{t}h=
constant. This constant must be
0
since
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}v+\sigma\partial_{t}h|_{t=0}=0
.

4.2.2
Construct the solution: Contraction Mapping Theorem

Now we need to verify

1.

\Xi
is a self-mapping of
\mathbb{X}
,

2.

\Xi
is a contraction on
\mathbb{X}
.

Once these two properties are proved, we can apply the Contraction Mapping Theorem to
\Xi
to get there exists a unique fixed point
(v,h,\eta)
of
\Xi
which solves the linearized system (
4.6
).

First we verify
\Xi
is a self-mapping of
\mathbb{X}
.

Estimates for
\eta
:

A direct computation gives

\displaystyle\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial\eta(0)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial(w+\mathring{\psi})\|_{L^{\infty}}\,dt\leq 1+\int_{0}^{T}\|w\|_{Z^{4}}+\|\partial\mathring{\psi}\|_{2}\,dt,

(4.89)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{2}\eta\|_{Z^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial^{2}\eta(0)\|_{Z^{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial^{2}(w+\mathring{\psi})\|_{Z^{2}}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|w\|_{Z^{4}}+\|\mathring{\psi}\|_{Z^{4}}\,dt,

(4.90)

\displaystyle\|\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{t}\eta\|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k+1}(w+\mathring{\psi})\|_{0}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|w\|_{Z^{4}}+\|\mathring{\psi}\|_{Z^{4}}\,dt.

(4.91)

Estimates for
v

: First we have for
l=1,2,3,4
:

\displaystyle\|v\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{0}\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(\tau)\|_{0}d\tau\right)\,dt\leq\|v_{0}\|_{0}+T\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+T\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{0}\,dt

(4.92)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{l}v\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{t}^{l}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{l}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{0}\,dt\leq\|\partial_{t}^{l}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{Z^{3}}\,dt~~~1\leq l\leq 4,

(4.93)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq g+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{\infty}}.

(4.94)

For the space-time derivatives, we also have

\displaystyle\|\partial^{4}v\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{0}\|_{4}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\partial^{4}v\|_{0}\,dt\leq\|v_{0}\|_{4}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial^{4}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{0}\,dt\leq\|v_{0}\|_{4}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{Z^{3}}\,dt,

(4.95)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{l}\partial^{4-l}v\|_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{t}^{l}\partial^{4-l}v\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{l}\partial^{4-l}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{0}\,dt\leq\|v(0)\|_{Z^{4}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi\|_{Z^{3}}\,dt.

(4.96)

Therefore,

\|v\|_{Z^{4}}\leq\|v_{0}\|_{Z^{4}}+T\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi(t),\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi(t)\|_{Z^{3}}\lesssim(1+T)\mathcal{P}_{0}+TM.

(4.97)

Estimates for
h
:

It suffices to estimate
\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{Z^{3}}
and
\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{Z^{3}}
via the wave equation of
h
, i.e., (
4.86
). Again we can apply the same method as in Section
4.1.3
to derive

\|\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{Z^{3}}+\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}h\|_{Z^{3}}\lesssim_{M}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta\|_{2},\|v\|_{Z^{4}},\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi(t),\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}\pi(t)\|_{Z^{3}},\|\partial_{t}h\|_{Z^{4}})\,dt.

(4.98)

Combining (
4.89
)-(
4.91
), (
4.97
) and (
4.98
), we obtain that the solution map
\Xi
is a self-map of
\mathbb{X}
after applying the Gronwall’s inequality.

Next we prove
\Xi:\mathbb{X}(M,T)\to\mathbb{X}(M,T)
is a contraction. Given
(w_{1},\pi_{1},\xi_{1}),~(w_{2},\pi_{2},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{X}(M,T)
and their images under
\Xi

(v_{1},h_{1},\eta_{1}),~(v_{2},h_{2},\eta_{2})
, we define

[w]:=w_{1}-w_{2},~[\pi]:=\pi_{1}-\pi_{2},~[\xi]:=\xi_{1}-\xi_{2};~~[v]:=v_{1}-v_{2},~[h]:=h_{1}-h_{2},~[\eta]:=\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}.

From (
4.84
), (
4.85
) and (
4.86
), we can derive the equations for
([v],[h],[\eta])
with initial data
(\mathbf{0},0,\mathbf{0})
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}[\eta]

\displaystyle=[w],

\displaystyle\partial_{t}[v]

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi],

\displaystyle\sigma\partial_{t}^{2}[h]-\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}}[h]

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}^{\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}[v]_{\alpha}-\partial_{t}\sigma\partial_{t}[h],~~~[h]|_{\Gamma}=0.

Similarly as above we can derive the estimates

\displaystyle\|\partial[\eta]\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial^{2}[\eta]\|_{Z^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}[\eta]\|_{Z^{3}}+\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi]\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi],\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi]\|_{Z^{3}}+\|[v]\|_{Z^{4}}

(4.99)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{M}\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial[\xi]\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial^{2}[\xi]\|_{Z^{2}},\|\partial_{t}[\xi]\|_{Z^{3}},\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi]\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi],\partial_{t}\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi]\|_{Z^{3}},\|[w]\|_{Z^{4}})\,dt.

Therefore, choosing
T_{\kappa}>0
sufficiently small such that RHS of (
4.99
) is bounded by

\frac{1}{2}\left(\|\partial[\xi]\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial^{2}[\xi]\|_{Z^{2}}+\|\partial_{t}[\xi]\|_{Z^{3}}+\|\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{a}}}[\pi]\|_{Z^{4}}+\|\partial_{t}[\pi]\|_{Z^{4}}+\|[w]\|_{Z^{4}}\right),

we prove that
\Xi:\mathbb{X}(M,T_{\kappa})\to\mathbb{X}(M,T_{\kappa})
is a contraction self-map. By the Contraction Mapping Theorem, we know
\Xi
has a unique fixed point
(v,h,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}(M,T_{\kappa})
which is the solution to the linearized approximation system (
4.6
).

4.3
Iteration and convergence of the solutions to the linearized system

Up to now we have constructed a sequence of solutions
\{(v^{(n)},h^{(n)},\eta^{(n)})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
which solves the
n
-th linearized
\kappa
-approximation system (
4.1
). The last step in this section is to prove that
\{(v^{(n)},h^{(n)},\eta^{(n)})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in some strong Sobolev norm, and thus produce a solution
(v,h,\eta)
to the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.1
).

Let
n\geq 3
, and define

[v]^{(n)}:=v^{(n+1)}-v^{(n)},~~[h]^{(n)}:=h^{(n+1)}-h^{(n)},~~[\eta]^{(n)}:=\eta^{(n+1)}-\eta^{(n)},

(4.100)

and

[a]^{(n)}:=a^{(n)}-a^{(n-1)},~~[\psi]^{(n)}:=\psi^{(n)}-\psi^{(n-1)}.

(4.101)

Then these quantities satisfy the following system with
vanishing initial data and no gravity term
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n)}=[v]^{(n)}+[\psi]^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
\partial_{t}[v]^{(n)}=-\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[h]^{(n)}-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}h^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}=-\text{div}_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}v^{(n)}-e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\partial_{t}[h]^{(n)}-(e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))\partial_{t}h^{(n)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
[h]^{(n)}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma\\
\end{cases}

(4.102)

We will prove the following energy converges to 0 as
n\to\infty
for all
t\in[0,T]

[\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t):=\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{3-k}[v]^{(n)}(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3-k}[h]^{(n)}(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\|[\eta]^{(n)}(t)\|_{3}^{2}+\|[a]^{(n)}(t)\|_{2}^{2}.

(4.103)

Remark
.

Since the gravity term has been cancelled in (
4.102
), we then could directly include the standard
H^{3}
Sobolev norm of
[h]
in
[\mathcal{E}]
instead of
\|\partial[h]\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial^{2}[h]\|_{1}^{2}
.

4.3.1
Estimates of
[a]
,
[\psi]
and
[\eta]

By definition, we have

\displaystyle[a]^{(n)\mu\nu}(T)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}(a^{(n)\mu\nu}-a^{(n-1)\mu\nu})\,dt

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T}[a]^{(n)\mu\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}\eta^{(n)}_{\gamma}a^{(n)\beta\nu}+a^{(n-1)\mu\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n-1)}_{\gamma}a^{(n)\beta\nu}+a^{(n-1)\mu\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}\eta^{(n-1)}_{\gamma}[a]^{(n)\beta\nu},

which gives

\|[a]^{(n)}(T)\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\|[a]^{(n)}(t)\|_{2}^{2}\|\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}\,dt\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\|[a]^{(n)}(t)\|_{2}^{2}(\|[v]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}+\|[\psi]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}))\,dt.

(4.104)

As for
[\psi]^{(n)}
, it satisfies
-\Delta[\psi]^{(n)}=0
subject to the following boundary condition

\displaystyle[\psi]^{(n)}=\overline{\partial}{}^{-1}\mathbb{P}\bigg(

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}{}[\eta]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v^{(n)}+\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{(n-1)}_{\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}v^{(n)}+\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}[v]^{(n-1)}

\displaystyle-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}[\eta]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v^{(n)}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta^{(n-1)}_{\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v^{(n)}-\overline{\Delta}{}\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\eta^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)i\beta}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}[v]^{(n-1)}\bigg).

By the standard elliptic estimates, we have the control for
[\psi]^{(n)}

\|[\psi]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim|[\psi]^{(n)}|_{2.5}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\left(\|[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}^{2}+\|[v]^{(n-1)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}\right).

(4.105)

Therefore, we obtain

\sup_{[0,T]}\|[a]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\left(\|[a]^{(n)},[a]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|[v]^{(n-1)},[v]^{(n-2)},[\eta]^{(n-2)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}^{2}\right),

(4.106)

and the bound for
[\eta]
combining with
\partial_{t}[\eta]^{(n)}=[v]^{(n)}+[\psi]^{(n)}
:

\sup_{[0,T]}\|[\eta]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\left(\|[a]^{(n)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|[v]^{(n)},[v]^{(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}^{2}\right)

(4.107)

Similar as in Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
4.2
, one can get estimates for the time derivatives of
[\eta]
and
[\psi]

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}\psi]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\left(\|[a]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n-1)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|[v]^{(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}^{2}\right)

(4.108)

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\psi]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\left(\|[a]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n-1)}\|_{1}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n-1)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|[v]^{(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{3}^{2}\right)

(4.109)

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}\eta]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\left(\|[a]^{(n)},[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)},[\partial_{t}v]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|[v]^{(n)},[v]^{(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}^{2}\right)

(4.110)

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}^{2}\eta]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\left(\|[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n),(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{1}}^{2}+\|[a]^{(n)},[\partial_{t}v]^{(n),(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|[v]^{(n),(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}^{2}\right)

(4.111)

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}^{3}\eta]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\left(\|[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n),(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{1}}^{2}+\|[a]^{(n)},[\partial_{t}v]^{(n),(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}+\|[v]^{(n),(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}^{2}\right).

(4.112)

4.3.2
Estimates of
[h]

Taking
\tilde{J}^{(n)}\text{div}_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}
in the second equation of (
4.102
), we get an analogous wave equation for
[h]
:

\displaystyle e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\tilde{J}^{(n)}\partial_{t}^{2}[h]^{(n)}-\partial_{\nu}(\tilde{E}^{\nu\mu}\partial_{\mu}[h]^{(n)})

\displaystyle=\tilde{J}^{(n)}\tilde{a}^{(n)\nu\alpha}\partial_{\nu}({[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu}}_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}h^{(n)})

(4.113)

\displaystyle-\tilde{J}^{(n)}\partial_{t}\left((e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})-e^{\prime}(h^{(n-1)}))\partial_{t}h^{(n)}\right)

\displaystyle-\tilde{J}^{(n)}(\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{(n)\nu\alpha})\partial_{\nu}[v]^{(n)}_{\alpha},

where
\tilde{E}^{\nu\mu}:=\tilde{J}^{(n)}\tilde{a}^{(n)\nu\alpha}{\tilde{a}^{(n)\mu}}_{\alpha}
.

One can apply the similar method in Section
3.3
and use the estimates of
[\eta],[\psi]
to obtain the following energy estimates

\sum_{k=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{k}[h]^{(n)}\|_{3-k}^{2}+\|\sqrt{e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})}\partial_{t}^{3}[h]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\left([\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)\right)\,dt.

(4.114)

4.3.3
The div-curl estimates

From Hodge’s decomposition inequality Lemma
2.7
, we have

\displaystyle\|[v]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{div }[v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\text{curl }[v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+|[v]^{(n)}\cdot N|_{2.5}

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{div }[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}+\|\text{curl }[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}+|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\cdot N|_{1.5}

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{div }[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{curl }[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\cdot N|_{0.5}

The
L^{2}
-norm can be bounded in the same way as in Section
3.4
and the boundary term can be reduced to the tangential estimates for
[v]
and its time derivative. As for the
curl
part, we apply
\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}
to the second equation of (
4.102
) to get the evolution equation of
\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}[v]^{(n)}}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}[v]^{(n)}})_{\lambda}=\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{(n)\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}[v]^{(n)}_{\alpha}-\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\nu\mu}\partial_{\nu}v^{(n)}_{\alpha}.

(4.115)

Applying
D^{2}=\partial^{2},\partial\partial_{t}
or
\partial_{t}^{2}
to (
4.115
), and mimicking the proof in Section
3.4
, one can get

\displaystyle\|\text{curl }[v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|[v]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\left(\|[v]^{(n),(n-1)},[\eta]^{(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}}+\|[\tilde{a}]^{(n),(n-1)}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}\right)

(4.116)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|[v]^{(n)}\|_{3}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\sup_{[0,T]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n),(n-1)}(t)

\displaystyle\|\text{curl }[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\sup_{[0,T]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n),(n-1)}(t)

\displaystyle\|\text{curl }[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\|[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\sup_{[0,T]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n),(n-1)}(t).

Similar results hold for
div
control by using the same method as in Section
3.4
, so we only list the result here

\displaystyle\|\text{div }[v]^{(n)}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\text{div }[\partial_{t}v]^{(n)}\|_{1}^{2}\|+\text{div }[\partial_{t}^{2}v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}

(4.117)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left([\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)\right).

4.3.4
Tangential estimates of
[\partial_{t}^{k}v]
for
k\geq 1

Let
\mathfrak{D}^{3}=\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2},\partial_{t}^{3}
. Using the same method as in Section
3.5
and Section
4.1.2
(Step 4), we can derive the estimates

\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}[v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}[h]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\,dt.

(4.118)

4.3.5
Tangential estimates of
[v]
: Alinhac’s good unknown

We adopt the same method as in Section
3.6
. For each
n
, we define the Alinhac’s good unknowns by

\mathbf{V}^{(n+1)}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v^{(n+1)}-\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}v^{(n+1)},~~\mathbf{H}^{(n+1)}=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}h^{(n+1)}-\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}h^{(n+1)}.

(4.119)

Their difference is denoted by

\displaystyle[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}:=\mathbf{V}^{(n+1)}-\mathbf{V}^{(n)},[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}:=\mathbf{H}^{(n+1)}-\mathbf{H}^{(n)}.

Similarly as in Section
3.6
, we can derive the analogous version of (
3.80
) as

\partial_{t}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}+\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}=-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}\mathbf{H}^{(n)}+\mathbf{F}^{(n)},

(4.120)

subject to the boundary data

[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}|_{\Gamma}=-\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)3\beta}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)3\beta}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)3\beta}\right),

(4.121)

and the compressibility equation

\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}=-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}\cdot\mathbf{V}^{(n)}+\mathbf{G}^{(n)},

(4.122)

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{(n)\alpha}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}[v]^{(n)}_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~+[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{a}^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}h^{(n+1)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}h^{(n+1)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}

\displaystyle~~~~+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}[h]^{(n)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}h^{(n)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}h^{(n+1)}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}h^{(n+1)}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}h^{(n+1)}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}[h]^{(n)}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}h^{(n+1)}\right]-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}[h]^{(n)}\right],

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(\text{div }_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}[v]^{(n)}-\text{div }_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}v^{(n)})

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha}-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{2},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{\gamma}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\partial_{\mu}[v]^{(n)}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle~~~~-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha}\right]-\left[\overline{\partial}{}^{3},\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\alpha},\partial_{\mu}[v]^{(n)}_{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle~~~~+[\tilde{a}]^{(n)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{a}^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}v^{(n+1)}_{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}

\displaystyle~~~~+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}(\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}[v]^{(n)}_{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}+\tilde{a}^{(n-1)\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}([\tilde{a}]^{(n)\gamma\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}v^{(n)}_{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}.

Multiplying
[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}
in (
4.120
) and integrate by parts in the
[\mathbf{H}]
term, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{V}^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}\left(\nabla_{\tilde{a}^{(n)}}\cdot[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}-\partial_{\mu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}_{\alpha}\right)\,dy+\int_{\Omega}(\mathbf{F}^{(n)}-\nabla_{[\tilde{a}]^{(n)}}\mathbf{H}^{(n)})\cdot[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}\,dy

\displaystyle~~~~-\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}\tilde{a}^{(n)3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}_{\alpha}\,dS.

Similarly as in (
3.84
)-(
3.89
), the first two integrals contribute to the energy term

-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}[h]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}.

modulo error terms that can be controlled by
\mathcal{P}_{0}([\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)})
.
As for the boundary term, we can mimic the proof in (
4.42
), i.e., integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{0.5}
by parts, to get

\displaystyle~~~~-\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{H}]^{(n)}\tilde{a}^{(n)3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}_{\alpha}\,dS

\displaystyle=\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h\tilde{a}^{(n)3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}_{\alpha}\left(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)3\beta}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[\tilde{\eta}]^{(n-1)}_{\beta}\tilde{a}^{(n)3\beta}+\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\tilde{\eta}^{(n-1)}_{\beta}[\tilde{a}]^{(n)3\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim|[\mathbf{V}]^{(n)}|_{\dot{H}^{-0.5}}\left(\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathcal{P}_{0}|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]^{(n-1)}|_{\dot{H}^{0.5}}+\|[\tilde{a}]\|_{2}\right).

Summing up all the estimates above and using the analogue of (
3.77
), we get

\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[v]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|e^{\prime}(h^{(n)})\overline{\partial}{}^{3}[h]^{(n)}\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left([\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\right).

(4.123)

Finally, we combine the estimates for
[\eta],[a],[h],[v]
and div-curl estimates above and obtain

[\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}T^{2}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left([\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\right).

Therefore by choosing
T=T_{\kappa}>0
sufficiently small (The time
T_{\kappa}
depends on
\kappa>0
because the uniform-in-
n
estimates depend on
\kappa^{-1}
), we can get

\sup_{[0,T_{\kappa}]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)\leq\frac{1}{8}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-1)}(t)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n-2)}(t)\right),

(4.124)

which implies

\sup_{[0,T_{\kappa}]}[\mathcal{E}]^{(n)}(t)\leq\frac{1}{2^{n}}\mathcal{P}_{0}\to 0~~~\text{as }n\to\infty.

4.4
Construction of the solution to the approximation system (
3.1
)

Proposition 4.3
.

Suppose the initial data
(v_{0},h_{0})
satisfying
\|v_{0}\|_{4}+\|h_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq M_{0}
and the compatibility conditions up to order
4
. Given
\kappa>0
, there exists a
T_{\kappa}>0
such that the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.1
) has a unique solution
(v(\kappa),h(\kappa),\eta(\kappa))
in
[0,T_{\kappa}]
satisfying the estimates

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim C(M_{0}),

(4.125)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(t)

\displaystyle:=\|\partial^{2}\eta(t)\|_{2}^{2}+\|\partial\eta(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}v\|_{k}^{2}+\left(\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}+\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}h\|_{k}^{2}\right)+W(t),

(4.126)

\displaystyle W(t)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{5-k}h(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{4-k}\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h(t)\|_{k}^{2}+\|\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\partial\nabla_{\tilde{a}}h\|_{3}^{2}.

Proof.

In Section
4.2
, we proved that the linearized system (
4.1
) admits a solution
(\eta^{(n+1)},v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)})
assuming that

(\eta^{(k)},v^{(k)},h^{(k)}),\,\,\,k\leq n

are known and satisfying (
4.5
).
Moreover, in light of (
4.81
) and (
4.124
), we obtain the strong convergence of the sequence of approximation solutions
\{(\eta^{(n+1)},v^{(n+1)},h^{(n+1)})\}
as
n\to+\infty
. The limit
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),h(\kappa))
solves the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.1
) and the energy estimate (
4.125
) is a direct consequence of the uniform-in-
n
estimate (
4.81
).
∎

5
Local well-posedness of the compressible gravity water wave system

From Proposition
4.3
, given
\kappa>0
, we have constructed a solution
(v(\kappa),h(\kappa),\eta(\kappa))
to the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.1
). Proposition
3.1
gives a
\kappa
-independent estimate (
3.4
) on some time interval
[0,T_{0}]
, which yields a strong convergence to a limit
(v,h,\eta)
for every
t\in[0,T_{0}]
. This limit
(v,h,\eta)
is a solution to the compressible gravity water wave system (
1.13
) with energy estimate (
1.17
) in Theorem
1.2
if we set
\kappa\to 0+
in (
3.1
). Therefore, the existence has been proved.

Let
(v^{1},h^{1},\eta^{1}),(v^{2},h^{2},\eta^{2})
be two solutions to the compressible gravity water wave system (
1.13
) with the initial data
(v_{0},h_{0})
and
(\hat{v}_{0},\hat{h}_{0})
, respectively. Denoting their difference by
([v],[h],[\eta]):=(v^{1}-v^{2},h^{1}-h^{2},\eta^{1}-\eta^{2})
and
a^{i}:=(\partial\eta^{i})^{-1}
with
[a]:=a^{2}-a^{1}
, then
([v],[h],[\eta])
solves the following system:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]=[v]~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}[v]=-\nabla_{a^{1}}[h]+\nabla_{[a]}h^{2}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }_{a^{1}}[v]=\text{div }_{[a]}v^{2}-e^{\prime}(h^{2})\partial_{t}[h]-(e^{\prime}(h^{1})-e^{\prime}(h^{2}))\partial_{t}h^{2}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
[h]=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
([\eta],[v],[h])|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{0},v_{0}-\hat{v}_{0},h_{0}-\hat{h}_{0}).\end{cases}

(5.1)

We define the energy functional of (
5.1
) by

[\mathcal{E}]=\|[\eta]\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2-k}[v]\|_{k}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2-k}[h]\|_{k}^{2}+|(a^{1})^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{\alpha}|_{0}^{2}.

(5.2)

This looks very similar to (
4.102
). The only essential difference is the boundary term

\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{H}](a^{1})^{3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]_{\alpha}\,dS,

where we define the Alinhac’s good unknowns

\mathbf{V}^{i}=\overline{\partial}{}^{2}v^{i}-\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{i}\cdot\nabla_{a^{i}}v^{i},~~\mathbf{H}^{i}=\overline{\partial}{}^{2}h^{i}-\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{i}\cdot\nabla_{a^{i}}h^{i},

and

[\mathbf{V}]:=\mathbf{V}^{1}-\mathbf{V}^{2},~~[\mathbf{H}]:=\mathbf{H}^{1}-\mathbf{H}^{2}.

The boundary terms then becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}[\mathbf{H}](a^{1})^{3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}[h]\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{\beta}(a^{2})^{3\beta}(a^{2})^{3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]_{\alpha}\,dS-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{\beta}(a^{1})^{3\beta}+\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{\beta}[a]^{3\beta})(a^{1})^{3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]_{\alpha}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}|(a^{1})^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{\alpha}|_{0}^{2}\,dS

\displaystyle~~~~-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}(a^{1})^{3\gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{\gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{\beta}[a]^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}v^{1}_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{\beta}(a^{2})^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}[v]_{\alpha})(a^{1})^{3\alpha}\,dS

\displaystyle~~~~-\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{3}h^{1}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{\beta}(a^{1})^{3\beta}+\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta^{2}_{\beta}[a]^{3\beta})(a^{1})^{3\alpha}[\mathbf{V}]_{\alpha}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{c_{0}}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma}|(a^{1})^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{\alpha}|_{0}^{2}\,dS+C(M_{0})P([\mathcal{E}](t)),

where
M_{0}
is the constant defined in Theorem
1.2
.
Here in the second step we use the precise formula of
[\mathbf{V}]
, and in the third step we apply the physical sign condition for
h^{1}
. Therefore we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T_{0}]}[\mathcal{E}](t)\leq P(\|v_{0}-\hat{v}_{0}\|_{2},\|h_{0}-\hat{h}_{0}\|_{2})+\int_{0}^{T_{0}}C(M_{0})P([\mathcal{E}](t))\,dt,

which implies (
1.18
). Also, when
v_{0}=\hat{v}_{0}
and
h_{0}=\hat{h}_{0}
, we know
[\mathcal{E}](t)=0
for all
t\in[0,T_{0}]
which gives the uniqueness of the solution to the compressible gravity water wave system (
1.13
).
